**Task 13 — the reader, lab and header residue, and the focus contract (D-8729).** One commit per finding; its rows are in
the fence below, every one measured in the end-state replay at the close (its `at` field).
- The policy ring, `44c695cb8`. `reader/policy.ts` (ring L1) imported `docLinkTarget` from `docsLinks.ts`, which imports
  `pinFor` from `useDocsVisit.ts`, a hook module. `reader-policy.test.ts` now walks every runtime import and re-export from
  `policy.ts` with the TypeScript compiler API (the shared kernel included) and fails, naming the chain, on a `.tsx`, a
  module with a runtime `react` import, a store or `lib/api.ts`. Red first: `Tests  1 failed | 74 passed (75)`, naming
  `policy.ts -> docsLinks.ts -> useDocsVisit.ts` and `lib/auth.ts`. The shared pure link decision (`docLinkTarget`, its
  `DocLinkTarget`, and `docsLocation` with `DocsPageTarget`) moved unchanged to `pwa/src/docs/docLinkTarget.ts`, which
  imports no hook module; `docsLinks.ts` re-exports the four names, so every importer keeps its path and each is defined
  once; no scan pins their home (`ledgers-console`'s pin on `docHref` stays in `docsLinks.ts`). The review's minor 2
  (`d6ab36910`): under the package's `verbatimModuleSyntax`, an import whose specifiers are each marked `type` still loads
  its module, so the walk now erases only `import type` and `export type`. Rows `NDR-D2-T13-WALK`,
  `NDR-D2-T13FR-WALK-INLINE-TYPE`.
- D-8729, the mount focus, `2354ba029` (with the same-page link below, which shares its files) and `a9ea5a717` (the browser
  rows). `policy.ts#readerMountFocusFor` answers the library's boolean `focus`: the reader lab always, the Docs reader
  unless a host element owns focus. `bridge.ts` reads `el.ownerDocument.activeElement` once per library mount, after the
  assets load and the Mermaid wait and in the same synchronous run as the mount call; a connected element other than the
  body and the document element, outside the reader's element, owns focus. The adapter key set is unchanged, and `command()`
  still focuses the frame at once. Red first: `Tests  1 failed | 67 passed (68)` against a stub rule, and
  `Tests  13 failed | 54 passed (67)` on the bridge (eight bridge cases were green on the old literal `true` and are
  evidence of the arms that answer true, not reds). The rich-docs spec's §4.1 `Focus` row, §6 `Keys` row and M8.1 are
  amended in place, each marked "(amended 2026-11-18; D2, D-8729)", M8.1 keeping its positive contract. In Chrome
  (`a9ea5a717`, the reader's bridge chunk held by a route that parks its request until the case releases it):
  docs-compact-head's eight first-Tab-stop cases (both skins, five and seven directories, 600 x 960 and 900 x 800 under a
  fine pointer) focus back by keyboard while the chunk is held, and after ready back still holds focus, the frame was not
  focused at insertion, and one Tab lands on the first crumb, seen whole; a phone bar door keeps its focus at 412 x 915;
  with nothing focused the frame takes focus at insertion and at ready, phone-born here and at 1280 in M8.1 live, unchanged;
  and with the Mermaid wait held, focus moved onto back during it keeps the frame unfocused while focus left before it lets
  the frame take it, so the decision reads focus after the last await. The phone-born case found a second defect: when the
  reader came up on ccrc's bar, the head swapped from `CompactHead` to `OneBar`, different components, and a back button
  focused while the chunk loaded was unmounted, so focus dropped to the page body. Asks 554 and 555 ruled the fix by
  structure (`05f261824`): one `PhoneHead` renders both phone states at the same place under the same key and owns the
  header, the row with `BackButton` first, the panel container with its open state, id and Escape handler, and the actions;
  the swap replaces only each state's controls and panel tail. Back is the same element and keeps focus, no `focus()` call
  was added, and one Tab reaches the one bar's title. The header's DOM is identical at the base and after the change in four
  states (row and bar, panel closed and open), React's generated ids compared as a pairing (**local**). Red first: the unit
  case (`expected <header> to be <header>`) and the Chrome case (`{ same: false, connected: false, back: true }`). The
  compact head's Share, Export and Page details, and a control inside the open panel, have no same-place counterpart in the
  one bar, so focus on one of them still drops at the swap: D-8730, defined with the change in Deviations found. The second
  review's minor 1 (`307cc4580`) draws the open panel's content anew at the swap, so focus is never left on a hidden
  control. One measured difference, the second review's nit 2: the tree-less compact head (shown while the tree loads) is
  now a component of its own, so its header node, the plain-view note and "Updated to" are drawn anew when the tree arrives,
  where the header node used to be kept (back was already drawn anew there); a screen reader may announce that status line
  again on a first load.
- The two focus mutations the plan names, in Chrome: restoring unconditional Docs mount focus
  (`NDR-D2-T13-FOCUS-ALWAYS-ON`) reds the held host-control cases (the eight desk-born, both phone-born and the held
  Mermaid case), and always disabling it (`-FOCUS-ALWAYS-OFF`) reds
  M8.1 live's focus assertions, the phone-born no-owner case and the case whose focus left before the Mermaid wait.
  Reading the focus before the assets load (`-FOCUS-EARLY`) reds both Mermaid cases, and a focus the library would take
  300 ms after ready (`NDR-D2-T13FR2-FOCUS-LATE`) reds the second look a moment after ready that the second review asked
  for (its minor 2, `b3f2f76ff`). The held rows stand for both timings: a hold only widens the window between the head's
  back appearing and the mount, and an unheld case cannot fix the order of the focus and the mount, so it could red under
  neither focus mutation (the second review's minor 3).
- A same-page link, `2354ba029`, with the review's fixes `16b432311` and `9a6d19fa6`. A link to this very page spelled with
  its file name (`this.md#sec`) reached the host as a navigate the frame had not scrolled and only rewrote the address.
  `routeReaderLink`'s `hash` arm now says `reopen` (L1: true for that link, false for a `#sec` link, which the frame scrolls
  itself through the library's own hash path), and `ReaderFrame` then calls the mount's `openAt(id)`: the bridge destroys
  the frame and mounts the same rendered document again with `initialHash`, reading no body, image or history, deciding
  focus anew and carrying the latest theme (the library's `update()` would keep the first mount's `focus`). The address is
  rewritten through the existing hash path, so no history entry is added. The visit holds the anchor until the frame opened
  at it is ready, so a fallback of that frame lands the plain view at it (the review's minor 3), and a mount destroyed while
  the library mounts the new frame tears that frame down too (the review's nit 6). The cost, ruled a consequence of the
  released library and not a departure (ask 553): library 1.1.0 has no host scroll op, so the jump is a remount, and the
  frame's view of the visit resets: the "Changed since your last visit" notice and its menu item do not come back, and open
  folds and shown change marks reset; the stored read marks, prefs and last visit carry over. The rich-docs §4.1 `Remount`
  row says so (`ba254d605`). For the library owner: a host scroll op, or a view state the frame keeps across a remount, in a
  later release (beside C114's maintenance, below). In Chrome (`1107bec51`): the link reopens the frame (the old iframe
  detached, a second one seen) with its target in view and the new frame focused at insertion, `history.length` unchanged,
  and no request for the document's file, its history or anything else. A bare `#id` link scrolls in the frame with no
  remount and no read (ask 553's first condition). The stored prefs, read marks and folds survive the remount and no stored
  key is lost; only the reading position and the last visit's time change, because the new frame records the visit again,
  with the same commit. The notice and change marks could not be observed reset: the fixture serves no real history. Four of
  the first rows (`NDR-D2-T13-OPENAT-NODESTROY`, `-DROPPED`, `-EVERY-HASH` and `-UNDECODED`) named lines the reviews' fixes
  rewrote, so the replay found them not applying; they are recorded on the lines those fixes left, each red. A proposed
  Chrome row that makes a `#sec` link reopen (`NDR-D2-T13-B-BARE`) survived: in Chrome a bare `#id` link is the library's
  own, handled in the frame, and never reaches the host's policy, so the browser case pins what a reader sees (no remount,
  no read) and the arm is held by the unit row `NDR-D2-T13-REOPEN-FRAGMENT`; the Chrome row is not recorded.
- The fragment, `e2e8de9c4`. `ReaderBody` spent the visit's pending fragment as soon as the library's mount returned,
  before the frame's `ready`, so a fallback raised after that (the watchdog, a forged ready, a second load) handed the
  plain view no fragment. It is now spent once, at this body's ready, never for a body that fell back or ended. Red first:
  `Tests  7 failed | 70 passed (77)`. An integration case renders the real `DocsScreen` and `DocsLeaf`, falls back after the
  mount resolved and finds the plain view scrolling the element the fragment names (no scroll on the old code).
  In Chrome (`1107bec51`): a forged ready with a wrong nonce makes the frame fall back after
  the mount (`nonce`), and the plain view scrolls the footnote the fragment names into view; without the fragment it stays
  below the fold.
- The image seed: already guarded. An image ref over the library's 2048-unit seed limit is left out before any plan or
  load (`reader/images.ts#READER_IMAGE_REF_MAX_UNITS`, C2 Task 8, rows `NDR-C2-T8-REF-LIMIT-CHECK` and `-VALUE`,
  `reader-images.test.ts`'s over-long ref cases), so the seed stays valid; a mount the library refuses falls back with the
  fixed note (`reader-frame.test.tsx`'s rejected-mount cases). No new test was needed for the unit arm. In Chrome
  (`1107bec51`): an image ref of `READER_IMAGE_REF_MAX_UNITS + 1` units, built from redundant `./` segments so that it
  names a real file, is left out: the reader comes up, the ordinary image decodes in the frame, and the long one shows the
  frame's not-loaded chip, with no read for its file.
- The copy check, the `hash` stray and the stray and meta-only controls: in Chrome (`1107bec51`), with the actual library.
  - The copy check: a block holding U+202E and U+200B opens the parent's "Copy with hidden characters?" sheet naming
    both, and the clipboard is not written until the sheet's own button, then once with the exact text. The vocabulary is
    the library's, not widened.
  - The `hash` stray: navigate, store.set and hash posted to the parent are counted as the parent's window receives them,
    and then none has an effect (no route, no store write, no sheet, `location.hash` unchanged and the frame not scrolled:
    the hash target sits far below the fold). The controls send the same three ops on the frame's own port, each in its own
    case, and each reaches its sink. No fixed delay is left in either half. The end-state replay found the stray case
    blind to one library defect: listening for the frame's window messages behind the usual source check
    (`NDR-D2-T13-B-STRAYWIN`, `1 passed`). The browser file's parent hook wraps `HTMLIFrameElement.prototype
    .contentWindow` in a Proxy to count posts to the frame, and a Proxy is never a message's source, so under the hook
    `event.source === iframe.contentWindow` never matches, as production's would. The case now runs with the wrapper off
    (`bareContentWindow`, `71dd65cd4`) and asserts that each stray message's source is the iframe's own window; the row
    is red, and so is a listener that checks only the origin (measured on a scratch copy of the tree).
  - The meta-only row's own control cannot be written. The frame's boot gate admits only `data:` sources, script hashes and
    `'none'` in the srcdoc meta, so every meta that would let the battery reach a network origin falls back: 20 metas
    measured with the `csp` attribute suppressed, where `connect-src *`, `img-src *`, an `img-src` naming origin A,
    `default-src *` and a dropped `default-src` all fall back with `error`, and only reorderings, a trailing semicolon and
    an extra script hash boot, each holding 0 of 27. Both halves are pinned, and the meta-only assertion still reds only
    through the boot gate, as W8a recorded. E18's limits stay as recorded: the battery's `iframe` channel cannot tell the
    frame's policy from the shell's inherited `frame-src 'none'` (so the meta-only row measures 26 channels against the
    frame's own policy), and the lab exercises no `store.set` and no same-source-only listener.
- The worker's chunk-load failure and the error names: retained. A render chunk that fails to load in the worker posts
  nothing, so the runner's budget answers the job `timeout` (W8a plan Task 4 Step 6, its "Carried, not fixed"; the chunk
  is precached); the guard is the runner's budget row `W8A-T4-M1`, re-recorded on the runner's shared core, where the
  budget timer now starts (`NDR-D2-RP-W8A-T4-M1`: with the timer removed, a render job no longer answers `timeout`).
  The three `errorName` copies
  (`markdownParse.ts`, `readerRenderJob.ts`, `highlightJob.ts`) are W8a Task 4's mandate: each keeps its worker's closure to
  its own modules, as `highlightJob.ts`'s header says; no repair, so no test was added.
- RL-b's two unpinned mutants, `5c592589c`, test only. The lab host's test spy now asserts the host is attached when it
  is scrolled, and a boundary row holds the busy frame's gap factor (2 399 ms own thread, 2 400 ms shared, in a 3 000 ms
  spin). Moving `hostIn`'s scroll above its append, and lowering the factor to 0.5, each passed the old suite (measured:
  `Tests  1 passed | 29 skipped (30)` and `Tests  31 passed (31)`) and red the new one; raising it to 0.9 reds too.
- RL-a, `efd6c8625`, comments and tests only: review 1468's F1 (the door's header comment now names, from the source, the
  armed box's `403 foreign-origin` for a write from origin `null` before the session check, and that a box serving the
  bundle has a catch-all for GET and HEAD only; the equal-answers case pins both; that comment's first wording hid eight
  lines from the census's lab scan, which the close's replay found and `9777b08d9` fixed), F2 (the never-read scan follows a
  probe handler however it is bound or imported and counts `req.socket`, `req.file()`, `req.parts()` and any computed key;
  the old scan missed all 15 new planted fixtures), and the test minors: the armed spies no longer print, a placement scan
  holds every `/api/reader-lab` route under `server/src` to `routes.ts`, the 40 fixed probe names are pinned one by one, an
  armed case with two real sign-ins pins owner separation, the entropy case shows the minted value reaches `runs.open` and
  the answer, and the equal-answers case compares headers. Declared: `close()` takes no shape check (a malformed nonce is a
  map miss) and no expiry sweep (an expired, unswept run closes for its own opener while `read` answers it absent; the PWA
  ignores the value), so neither is behavioural; the malformed-escape `400` has no row (Fastify answers it while routing,
  before any file of the door runs; the equal-answers case pins it). Carried, in files other sessions held when this run
  opened: the F4 matcher's `createRequire` and `import x = require()` (`box-token-census.test.ts`'s `FS_LOAD`), the
  `LAB_FILES` docstring (the same file), and the "a run a session opened" comments (`server.ts`, `auth-gate.test.ts`). The
  reader-lab plan's A5 to A7 errata and the historical row `RLB-T4-M2` are the coordinator's to fold.
- W8a review 1318's smaller items (asks 556, 557 and 559), each at its smallest: the sheets' header comments say three
  sheets, link, copy and copy-failed (`26739b114`); `assets.ts` says M7.9 admits the `?raw` asset form at any import
  position under `pwa/src` (`017d7a46e`); the window-message scan's `window` and `globalThis` owners each have a planted
  control, red when its owner is dropped (`ef3a4ffe2`); M7.9's known evasions name JSX text (`c197b243a`). The diffs of the
  three source files touch comment lines only. `docs.css`'s own "two sheets" comment stays: no CSS changes in D2.
- The phone header's residue: fresh counts, measured on D2's tree: `PHH-T4-M8` 2 failed and `PHH-FR-M6` 2 failed,
  as review 1463 counted; `PHH-T4-M9` 7 failed, re-recorded as `NDR-D2-RP-PHH-T4-M9` because D2's shell moved its line;
  `PHH-T4-M6` does not apply since #2480 removed its line from `DocsHead.tsx`. The phone-header plan's historical counts are
  not rewritten. D-7958's text (that Task 1's code fence carries the re-open) and the finding wording (F5, F6) are that
  plan's text, the coordinator's to fold. The fallback-only retry is as designed: `openLeaf` re-opens only on a `fallback`,
  and a wave that widens it carries the predicate.
- Lab 10: measured (ask 551, its six conditions held). `pwa/browser/app.ts`'s `AppView` gains an optional
  `serviceWorkers: 'allow'`; absent, a context blocks workers exactly as before, and a unit case in `browser-dist.test.ts`
  pins the default by behaviour (`openApp` over a stand-in browser asks for `'block'`, and `'allow'` only when told) and
  that exactly one browser file opts in. In an opted-in context the positive control comes first: the built app's own worker
  is one registration at scope `/`, activated, controlling the console page, and a precached fetch comes back from the
  worker. Then, in the reader's sandboxed srcdoc frame and in a hand-built `allow-scripts` child, `navigator.serviceWorker`
  throws `SecurityError`; a hand-built same-origin child does reach the controller, so the probe is not blind. The reader
  frame makes no request at all (two CSP violations), and the hand-built child's fetches and image load are answered by the
  network, not the worker. The shell's `frame-src 'none'` stops any child of the console from navigating, so the navigation
  pair runs in a same-origin host page the case serves: a plain iframe's navigation is the worker's, and a sandboxed
  iframe's is the network's. The context's routes still see what the worker forwards (the probe's `/api` request is
  asserted). A last block, that a fresh context holds no registration, is true by the harness's construction and is no
  evidence (fix round 1, A5; corrected in place). Rows: the app's worker registration removed (`NDR-D2-T13-B-LABREG`, so
  the case cannot pass on an inactive worker), the frame's sandbox widened (`-LABSBOX`), and the default flipped
  (`-LABDEFAULT`). The child is not controlled, so there was no finding to stop on.

{"id": "NDR-D2-T13FR-WALK-INLINE-TYPE", "pkg": "pwa", "file": "test/reader-policy.test.ts", "old": "      if (n.importClause?.isTypeOnly !== true) out.push(n.moduleSpecifier.text);", "new": "      const c = n.importClause;\n      const bindings = c?.namedBindings;\n      const allNamedType = c !== undefined && c.name === undefined && bindings !== undefined && ts.isNamedImports(bindings)\n        && bindings.elements.length > 0 && bindings.elements.every((e) => e.isTypeOnly);\n      if (!(c?.isTypeOnly === true || allNamedType)) out.push(n.moduleSpecifier.text);", "tests": ["test/reader-policy.test.ts"], "t": "still loads its module", "status": "red", "red": "Tests  1 failed | 76 skipped (77): an import or export whose specifiers are each marked type still loads its module (verbatimModuleSyntax keeps the empty import)", "sha256": "8deb3d0bae49028dbb04e30b81b50b2b5afd6b5e918e5d3666c0d81c99f07ded", "restored": true, "at": "ad47d42d7"}

**Task 13's reviews** (Opus, launched at high; the close's routing line says what each reported).
- The first (`44c695cb8` to `e2e8de9c4`): no critical finding. Important 1, the same-page remount resets the frame's view
  of the visit, went to the coordinator as ask 553, ruled a consequence of the released library: the cost is recorded
  above and in the rich-docs Remount row (`ba254d605`), with a request to the library owner. Minor 2 (`d6ab36910`, the walk
  counts an import whose specifiers are all `type`), minor 3 (`16b432311`, the visit holds a same-page anchor until the
  reopened frame is ready), minor 4 (the Remount row, `ba254d605`), nit 5 (`16b432311`, a non-null decoder) and nit 6
  (`9a6d19fa6` and `34bb23ce0`, `openAt` re-entrancy) are fixed, each with its row.
- The second (`a9ea5a717`, the fixes above and `05f261824`): no critical or important finding. Minor 1 (`307cc4580`, the
  open panel's content drawn anew at the swap; D-8730's text names it), minor 2 and nit 3 (`b3f2f76ff`, the focus rows look
  again 800 ms after ready, as M8.1 live does, and the phone back case waits for the tree's row), nit 1 (`63ef7e582`, the
  Remount row says the remount is the library frame's, within the same React mount) are fixed; minor 3 (no unheld focus
  row) and nit 2 (the tree-less head drawn anew when the tree arrives) are answered and recorded above.

**Files outside Task 13's Modify list**, each claimed before its edit:
- `pwa/src/docs/docLinkTarget.ts`, new: the plan's own "extract only the shared pure link decision from docsLinks".
- `pwa/src/docs/DocsHead.tsx` and `pwa/test/docs-compact-head.test.tsx`: the phone-head shell (asks 554 and 555, D-8730).
- `pwa/browser/app.ts` and `pwa/test/browser-dist.test.ts`: lab 10's opt-in and the pin of its default (ask 551).
- `pwa/src/docs/ReaderSheets.tsx`, `pwa/src/docs/reader/readerText.ts` and `pwa/src/docs/reader/assets.ts` (comment lines
  only), `pwa/test/docs-scans.test.ts` and `server/test/docs-parity.test.ts`: W8a's smaller items (asks 556, 557 and 559).
- The first item's files are the brief's (`DocImage.tsx`, `docs-images.test.tsx`); `docs-scans.test.ts` also carries the
  re-recorded `W5P1-T9-M19`. The RL-a files (`server/src/readerLab/routes.ts`, `server/test/reader-lab-routes.test.ts`,
  `server/test/reader-lab-vocabulary.test.ts`) and `pwa/test/reader-lab-run.test.ts`/`reader-lab-verdicts.test.ts` are in
  the list (RL-a's after the claim check the plan asks for).

**The close of D2 (Task 14).**

  started at the session's own effort, and it was stopped in its first step and launched again at high. Both Task 13
  reviewers' own reports named their effort as low although each was launched at high; recorded as they said.

    (34 passed); it is green in the run above. So D2's suite line is red.

{"id": "W8A-T11-M1", "pkg": "pwa", "file": "src/docs/docs.css", "old": "/* §4.1: the reader is sized to the Docs pane and the frame scrolls itself. A Docs page that holds the reader is one\n   pane tall: the shell is a viewport-tall column (the desktop grid already is), the pane and the screen and the leaf are\n   columns in turn, and the frame takes what the chrome above it leaves (never below `min-block-size`, where the pane\n   scrolls again). Every other Docs page, the plain view and the fallbacks keep the pane's own scrolling. */\n.app-shell:has(.docs-reader) { height: 100dvh; }\n.app-shell:has(.docs-reader) > .shell-detail { display: flex; flex-direction: column; min-block-size: 0; }\n@media (max-width: 899.98px) {\n  .app-shell:has(.docs-reader) { display: flex; flex-direction: column; }\n  .app-shell:has(.docs-reader) > .shell-detail { flex: 1 1 auto; }\n}\n.shell-detail > .docs-screen:has(.docs-reader) { display: flex; flex-direction: column; flex: 1 1 auto; min-block-size: 0; }\n.docs-screen:has(.docs-reader) > * { flex: none; }\n.docs-screen:has(.docs-reader) > .docs-leaf { display: flex; flex-direction: column; flex: 1 1 auto; min-block-size: 0; gap: var(--sp-2); }\n.docs-leaf:has(.docs-reader) > .doc-formatting { flex: none; }\n/* The frame fills its host by position, and the host takes the room by `flex-basis: 0`, so neither a percentage height\n   nor an auto flex basis has to resolve: the same in every engine. */\n.docs-reader { position: relative; flex: 1 1 0; inline-size: 100%; min-block-size: 20rem; }\n.docs-reader iframe { position: absolute; inset: 0; border: 0; inline-size: 100%; block-size: 100%; }\n", "new": ".docs-reader { inline-size: 100%; block-size: calc(100dvh - var(--sp-12)); min-block-size: 20rem; }\n.docs-reader iframe { border: 0; inline-size: 100%; block-size: 100%; }\n", "tests": ["browser/reader-frame.browser.test.ts"], "t": "sized to the Docs pane", "browser": true, "status": "red", "red": "Tests  1 failed | 53 skipped (54): the reader is sized to the Docs pane and only the frame scrolls, at 1280x800 and 390x844", "sha256": "f5a41a031a9f615ad1d9ffbff6fba9f06d83116eba4e6a98077367b8f66d3f51", "restored": true, "at": "71dd65cd4", "recorded": "2026-10-31-native-docs-reader-w8a-reader-frame-core.md:1865"}

{"id": "RLB-T3-M9", "pkg": "server", "file": "src/readerLab/routes.ts", "old": "app.get('/api/reader-lab/probe/:nonce/:name', { exposeHeadRoute: false }, async (_req, reply) => probeAnswer(reply));", "new": "app.options('/api/reader-lab/probe/:nonce/:name', async (_req, reply) => reply.code(204).header('access-control-allow-origin', '*').header('access-control-allow-headers', '*').send()); app.get('/api/reader-lab/probe/:nonce/:name', { exposeHeadRoute: false }, async (_req, reply) => probeAnswer(reply));", "tests": "test/reader-lab-routes.test.ts", "status": "red", "red": "Tests  3 failed | 18 passed (21): the probe path never reads a body: no body, raw-stream, socket or multipart access in the counter, either registration or any declaration they name (refinement (f)), every /api/reader-lab route registration under server/src is in readerLab/routes.ts, and those are the five doors (M10.8), a CORS preflight to the probe is never approved: no CORS header, a refusal, and nothing counted, dark or armed", "sha256": "0cdd77d803a1366fd4cfb38a2af7d75d8bef1ecfdc992d434f8b099a50e17bec", "restored": true, "at": "9777b08d9", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5229"}

{"id": "RLB-T3-M14", "pkg": "server", "file": "src/readerLab/routes.ts", "old": "const nonce = randomBytes(16).toString('base64url');", "new": "const nonce = Array.from({ length: 22 }, () => 'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789_-'[Math.floor(Math.random() * 64)]).join('');", "tests": "test/reader-lab-routes.test.ts", "status": "red", "red": "Tests  2 failed | 19 passed (21): a nonce is 128 bits from node:crypto: minted by randomBytes(16), imported from node:crypto, with no Math.random in the door (M10.2), the minted value is what the answer carries and what the store opens the run under: 16 bytes from randomBytes, base64url, minted once (M10.2)", "sha256": "0cdd77d803a1366fd4cfb38a2af7d75d8bef1ecfdc992d434f8b099a50e17bec", "restored": true, "at": "9777b08d9", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5234"}
{"id": "RLB-T3-M15", "pkg": "server", "file": "src/readerLab/routes.ts", "old": "    if (req.routeOptions.url !== READER_LAB_PROBE_ROUTE) return;", "new": "    const planted = /^\\/api\\/reader-lab\\/probe\\/([^/]+)/.exec(req.url); if (req.method === 'HEAD' && planted !== null && runs.read(planted[1]!, labOwnerOf(req.headers.cookie), Date.now()) !== null) throw Object.assign(new Error('planted'), { statusCode: 204 });\n    if (req.routeOptions.url !== READER_LAB_PROBE_ROUTE) return;", "tests": "test/reader-lab-routes.test.ts", "status": "red", "red": "Tests  1 failed | 20 passed (21): a request that reaches no probe handler gets one answer whether or not a run exists: a HEAD, a wrong-shaped path, a malformed escape, an over-long name, dark or armed (refinement (f))", "sha256": "0cdd77d803a1366fd4cfb38a2af7d75d8bef1ecfdc992d434f8b099a50e17bec", "restored": true, "at": "9777b08d9", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5235"}

{"id": "RLB-T3-M23", "pkg": "server", "file": "src/readerLab/routes.ts", "old": "  app.get('/api/reader-lab/probe/:nonce/:name', { exposeHeadRoute: false }, async (_req, reply) => probeAnswer(reply));", "new": "  app.post('/api/reader-lab/probe/*', async (req, reply) => { const rest = String((req.params as Record<string, unknown>)['*']); const live = runs.read(rest.split('/')[0]!, labOwnerOf(req.headers.cookie), Date.now()) !== null; return reply.code(rest.length > 150 && live ? 409 : 404).send(); });\n  app.get('/api/reader-lab/probe/:nonce/:name', { exposeHeadRoute: false }, async (_req, reply) => probeAnswer(reply));", "tests": "test/reader-lab-routes.test.ts", "status": "red", "red": "Tests  2 failed | 19 passed (21): every /api/reader-lab route registration under server/src is in readerLab/routes.ts, and those are the five doors (M10.8), a request that reaches no probe handler gets one answer whether or not a run exists: a HEAD, a wrong-shaped path, a malformed escape, an over-long name, dark or armed (refinement (f))", "sha256": "0cdd77d803a1366fd4cfb38a2af7d75d8bef1ecfdc992d434f8b099a50e17bec", "restored": true, "at": "9777b08d9", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5243"}

{"id": "RLB-T8-M6", "pkg": "pwa", "file": "src/readerLab/device.ts", "old": "return { engine, os, cores: typeof cores === 'number' && Number.isSafeInteger(cores) && cores > 0 ? cores : null, ...features };", "new": "return { engine: `${engine} ${ua}`, os, cores: typeof cores === 'number' && Number.isSafeInteger(cores) && cores > 0 ? cores : null, ...features };", "tests": ["test/reader-lab-verdicts.test.ts"], "status": "red", "red": "Tests  2 failed | 30 passed (32): deviceOf keeps classes only: engine and major version, OS class, cores, and the three features, the block is format 1: fixed keys in order, each line's words, the budget, the gaps, the reading and the door line, and no host, nonce or user agent (M10.14)", "sha256": "83b3e0086b75a4d2953291a89c173cd90bc35ef89433deafb2b7b30f26e2ffe2", "restored": true, "at": "71dd65cd4", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5564"}
{"id": "RLB-T8-M7", "pkg": "pwa", "file": "src/readerLab/verdicts.ts", "old": "const figures = local.mounts.filter((m) => /^f\\d$/.test(m.label) || m.label === 'all');", "new": "const figures = local.mounts.filter((m) => (/^f\\d$/.test(m.label) || m.label === 'all') && m.outcome === 'ready');", "tests": ["test/reader-lab-verdicts.test.ts"], "status": "red", "red": "Tests  2 failed | 30 passed (32): mermaid: a mount that fell back or timed out still counts its gap; within the budget it is not measured, never holds (F4), a dropped Mermaid asset is not measured even if the mount later says ready", "sha256": "ee900a78f2c8871a41e5d7684d63976e2f9921dd667472f8fc0ffee3533df7c6", "restored": true, "at": "71dd65cd4", "recorded": "2026-11-03-native-docs-reader-reader-lab.md:5565"}

**Fix round 1 (review run 2480, round 1 of 3, at `3145d75c6`), one commit per item.** The review found one important
finding (F1) and eight advisory notes (A1 to A8), measured the census gap the end-state replay had found, and kept no
other finding past its panel. The coordinator's rulings (mails 24497 and 24509): F1, the census fix and the minors in
D2; A4, A7 and A8 recorded with no change; and the commands clause made uniform in the spec. No subagent ran in this
round.
- F1 (important), `26fe68e81`. Task 13's second review's minor 1 (`307cc4580`) keyed both the crumbs and the truth line
  in the open Page details panel by the head's state, and its message says a unit case pins it. That case focuses a
  crumb, so it pinned the crumbs' key only: with the truth line's key removed, the review found both head files green.
  Two cases now focus the truth line's own controls in the open panel and swap the head to the one bar: the copy-commit
  button on a leaf, and Back to the latest on a version page. Each control is drawn anew, so focus is left on no hidden
  control. Removing the truth line's key (`NDR-D2-FR1-F1-TRUTH-KEY`) reds both and leaves the crumb case green. Removing
  the crumbs' key (`NDR-D2-T13FR2-PANEL-KEY`) reds the crumb case alone. So each half of the panel's key has its own
  red, and D-8730's "a control inside the open Page details panel" is pinned for the crumbs and the truth line. The
  panel's tail (GitHub, Open committed version) is `CompactPanelTail` in the row and `ActionsRow` in the bar. They are
  two components, so React draws the tail anew at the swap, with no key to pin. `307cc4580`'s message stays as history.
- The census item, `cc38442b5` (the coordinator's ruling: fix it in D2, since Task 13 owns the RL-a scanner). When the
  round began, run 2459 of another programme held the census file. Its worker released the file on request (mail 24506),
  and it was claimed before the edit. `labCode` in `server/test/box-token-census.test.ts` is now one left-to-right pass
  over strings, block comments and line comments. Whichever opens first wins: a string is kept whole and a comment is
  blanked, so a `/*` inside a line comment or inside a string opens nothing. A `//` right after a colon, a quote, a
  backtick or a backslash is still not a comment. Nothing else the census accepts changed. On both lab files at the tip,
  the scan's output equals the old one's, character for character, so no lab file reds. On `routes.ts` as `71dd65cd4`
  left it, the scan reads 49 non-blank code lines where the old scan read 41: the eight lines that `RLB-T4-M3` and `-M4`
  found hidden. The planted controls are a `/*` inside a line comment and a `"/*"` string. Each is followed by a guarded
  call (a log line, an fs import) and a later `*/`, and both must be seen. Restoring the old order
  (`NDR-D2-FR1-CENSUS-LAB-ORDER`) reds the case, on its first assertion, the line-comment control, so the case's report
  never reaches the string control. Dropping only the string alternatives (`-LAB-STRINGS`) reds it on the string control
  alone. Fix round 2 makes each control its own case: the order row then reds all four, and the string row the three
  string cases (below; corrected in place). The close's two sentences that left this to the coordinator are corrected in
  place.
- A1, `9ffa9aac5`. `onFragmentConsumed`'s doc now says what `onReady` does. It is called at the first `ready` when the
  mount was handed the fragment, and at the `ready` of each frame a same-page link reopened at its anchor, whether or
  not the mount was handed one. So it can run more than once for a body, and its one handler (`DocsLeaf`'s
  `setFragment(null)`) bears a repeat. Comment only.
- A2, `64269b62b`. `DocsHead.tsx`'s header comment now names `PhoneHead` and its two states: `CompactControls` and
  `CompactPanelTail` for the row, and `OneBarControls` for the bar. It names `CompactHead` as the head drawn while the
  tree is not `ok`, and no longer names the retired `OneBar`. `DOCS_COMPACT_QUERY`'s doc named `CompactHead` for the row
  and is corrected with it. `pwa/src/docs/docs.css` is not touched, so both 2026-11-06 phone blocks stay byte for byte.
  Its comment on the one-bar block still says the block is "drawn by `OneBar`", a component that no longer exists (the
  bar is `PhoneHead` with `OneBarControls`). That comment is recorded here, not changed.
- A3, `6a5bc8506`. The policy ring walk now reports a runtime import of `@ccrc/ui`, with its chain. The design system is
  consumed as source, and its one entry loads its React components. A planted control finds such an import one hop down
  (`policy.ts` to `links.ts`, with the specifier as the second step; fix round 2, F6 (c), corrected in place), and finds
  nothing for an `import type` of it. Removing the branch (`NDR-D2-FR1-A3-UI-BREAK`) reds that control, and the real
  walk still finds nothing.
- A4, recorded with no change. The "one base64 owner under `src/docs`" census in `docs-images.test.tsx` matches the text
  `btoa(` only, so a second encoder written another way would pass it. The first item's substitution itself
  (`DocImage.tsx` takes `base64Of` from `mockup/load.ts`) is pinned by eight characterization cases and replays red
  (`NDR-D2-FI-C2DUP-COPY`).
- A5, recorded with no assertion removed. Lab 10's last block, "a fresh context holds no registration", cannot red.
  `openApp` opens every context with `browser.newContext()`, an isolated profile, so a fresh one holds no registration
  whatever the closed one did. The block states a fact of the harness's construction: it is not a guard and not
  evidence. Task 13's lab 10 note above no longer counts it (corrected in place). The lab 10 evidence is the positive
  control and the srcdoc child after it.
- A6, plan text and a re-run. Four end-state rows (`RLB-T3-M6`, `W5HB-T3-M1`, `-M2` and `-M3`) spell `tests` as a
  space-separated string. The replay's runner read only the first file of such a string, and so did the enumeration. The
  first item's enumeration sentence now names that spelling. Read that way, its counts are unchanged, because no row's
  later file is one D1 changed. The four rows ran again at `9f62acced` on all their files, each red: `RLB-T3-M6` 4
  failed and 363 passed of 367, `W5HB-T3-M1` 3 failed and 234 passed of 237, `-M2` 4 failed and 224 passed of 228 and
  `-M3` 4 failed and 224 passed of 228. Their lines in the end-state fence above carry those counts. Read the same way,
  the close's own definition also held two rows that the first-token reading missed, `W5HA-T5-M5` and `W5HA-T6-M4`
  (their second file is `docs-scans.test.ts`, which D2 changed). They are in the fence below.
- A7, recorded with no change. `5b130be95`'s message says "the walk's 76 cases pass", but 76 is the file's case count.
  The walk's own block is 8 of them (`NDR-D2-T13-WALK`: 1 failed, 7 passed, 68 skipped, of 76; with A3's control it is
  now 9 of 77). The message stays as history.
- A8, recorded with no change. The three `sleep(800)` second looks in the focus rows of `reader-frame.browser.test.ts`
  (the desk-born, phone door and phone back cases) and the 20 ms `settled()` in `reader-frame.test.tsx` are bounded
  second looks for "nothing took focus later". They are not lab controls, which use counters and bounded polls.
  `NDR-D2-T13FR2-FOCUS-LATE` shows that a focus taken 300 ms after ready is caught.
- The spec, `9f62acced`. The §6 Keys row and M8.1's contract now say "the reader's own commands still focus the frame",
  as §4.1's Focus row already said of the reader's tools, under the same "(amended 2026-11-18; D2, D-8729)" mark. D-8729
  and Task 13 say all three amended sentences carry that clause.
- Rows. The four new rows, and every end-state row whose test file this round changed, ran at `9f62acced` (55 rows: 28
  on `reader-policy.test.ts`, 21 on `docs-compact-head.test.tsx` and 6 on the census). All 59 are red: the 55 rows'
  lines above carry the new counts, and the four new rows are in the fence below. The census change also brings eight
  earlier rows into the close's definition, rows whose tests read the census and that apply at the tip: `W3-T8-M4`,
  `-M7`, `-M8`, `-M9`, `-M10`, `-M11` and `-M13`, and `W5B-T10-M1`. With A6's two, these ten ran at `9f62acced` too:
  each red, with its counts in the fence below. So the end-state table now holds 564 rows (the close's 550, this round's
  four and these ten), and by each row's last run all 564 are red. Fix round 2 finds that the definition held 25 more,
  and brings the table to 592 (below; corrected in place).
- Suites at the round's code tip `9f62acced`:
  - pwa `tsc --noEmit` rc 0 (it covers `src`, `test` and `browser`);
  - every docs and reader unit file (`test/docs-*` and `test/reader-*`) and `browser-dist.test.ts`: 49 files, 1 981
    passed;
  - the census and the reader lab door's own server files (reader-lab-routes, -runs and -vocabulary, box-token-census,
    auth-gate, scan-symbols): 6 files, 462 passed;
  - D1's server guard set: rings, single-definition, census-w7 and census-kernel, 52 files, 1 712 passed; and
    deviation-refs after a fetch of `origin/main` at `b3be4f3a9`, anchors, citations, growth-caps, growth-check, the
    consolidation census and path guard, ci-ledger-guard, ledger-crosstree, ownership, dtbd, oss-metadata, public-text,
    topology-clean, docs-parity, no-default-saving-keystroke and crossrepo-prose, 17 files, 429 passed and 3 skipped;
    the plan-text guards run again after this commit;
  - `reader-frame.browser.test.ts` alone, five times (desktop Chromium `chromium-1248`, **local**): 54 passed each time,
    nothing skipped and no watchdog fallback, at a one-minute load average between 9.2 and 13.7 (read before and after
    each run).

**Fix round 2 (review run 2557, round 2 of 3, at `36a11a4bb`), one commit per item.** The review held fix round 1's
F1, the census scan and A1 to A8, and found two important findings (F1, F2) and four minors (F3 to F6). The
coordinator's rulings (mail 24907): F1, F3, F5 and F6 fixed; F2, the table completed to its own definition, its text not
narrowed; F4, no new reds, and D-8730's pinned and descriptive parts listed here. No subagent ran in this round.
- F1 (important), `ad47d42d7`. The lab scan keeps three kinds of string whole (single-quoted, double-quoted and
  template), but only the double-quoted one had a planted control, so dropping the single-quote or the template arm left
  the census green (the review measured 61 of 61 each). The controls are now four cases of one table, one per shape: a
  `/*` inside a line comment, a double-quoted string, a single-quoted string and a template literal, each followed by a
  guarded call (a log line or an fs load) and a later `*/`. Each reports on its own, which also settles F6 (b). Measured
  at `ad47d42d7`: restoring the old order (`NDR-D2-FR1-CENSUS-LAB-ORDER`) reds all four cases (4 failed, 6 passed and 54
  skipped of 64); dropping the three string arms (`-LAB-STRINGS`) reds the three string cases (3 failed, 7 passed and 54
  skipped of 64); and dropping only the single-quote arm (`NDR-D2-FR2-CENSUS-LAB-SQ`), only the double-quote arm
  (`-LAB-DQ`) or only the template arm (`-LAB-TPL`) reds that arm's case alone (each 1 failed, 9 passed and 54 skipped
  of 64). The three new rows are in the fence below.
- F2 (important), the coordinator's ruling: the end-state table is completed to its own definition. The enumeration, so
  that round 3 can reproduce it:
  - plans: every file under `docs/superpowers/plans`, recursively, read at the code tip `ad47d42d7` (920 files, every
    one Markdown);
  - rows: every JSON object with a string `id`, in either spelling (one object per line, or objects in an indented JSON
    array), found by trying a JSON decode at every `{` and skipping what a kept object contains;
  - a mutation row has `old` and `new` strings, or `edits`: a list of `{file, old, new}` objects or `[old, new]` pairs
    (a pair, or an object without a file, edits the row's own `file`); a row with `edits` is read by its edits;
  - an id's latest recording is its last mutation recording, plans in path order, then by position (4 352 recordings of
    3 105 ids);
  - a file is read as a repository path and with the row's `pkg` prefix, each with `..` folded (a package-relative
    `../shared/docs.ts` is `shared/docs.ts`), and the reading that exists at the tip is the file (no row had both); a
    file under `node_modules`, the installed library, is read from the work tree;
  - `tests` is read as an array, a comma-separated string or a space-separated string (each token ending in `.ts`,
    `.tsx`, `.mjs` or `.js`), each token read the same two ways;
  - in scope: a file or a test, in either reading, is one of D2's 28 changed files (`git diff --name-only
    8ff15803e..ad47d42d7`); applies: every edit's `old` occurs exactly once in its file at the tip;
  - the table: every id whose last recording in this plan carries an `at` of `71dd65cd4`, `9777b08d9` or `9f62acced`
    (564 ids, all applying).
  - The result: 633 rows in scope (124 of D1 and D2, 509 earlier), 534 of them applying, and 25 that apply and were not
    in the table: the review's 24 and one more. The 24 are the 22 that list `docs-parity.test.ts` (changed by
    `c197b243a`) among their tests, the W1 plan's `R2-ts`, `R2-py`, `R3-py`, `R3-ts`, `R4a`, `R6c`, `R48a`, `R48b`,
    `R48c`, `R58`, `R63a`, `R63b`, `R7-12`, `M3.12`, `M7.3a` and `M7.3b` and the W4 plan's `W4-T9-M6`, `-M7`, `-M8`,
    `W4-T10-M10`, `-M11` and `-M12`; `RLB-T3-M19`; and `S44`, whose test is the census, which fix round 1 changed. The
    25th is `RLB-T3-ORDER`, the reader lab plan's order row: its test is `reader-lab-routes.test.ts`, which D2 changed,
    and its two edits are `[old, new]` pairs on `server.ts` with no `old` and `new` of its own, so neither the close's
    reading nor the review's found it.
  - Why the close missed them: its enumeration read a file written from the repository root (`shared/docs.ts`,
    `ccd/ccd`, `deploy/deploy.sh`, `CLAUDE.md`, `pwa/src/lib/sw-denylist.ts`, each in a row whose `pkg` is `server`)
    with the package prefix, found no such file and counted the row as not applying, and it did not read `[old, new]`
    pairs.
  - All 25 ran at `ad47d42d7`: the 24 rows other than `W4-T9-M7` each red as recorded (their counts in the fence below),
    and `W4-T9-M7` as re-recorded below.
  - `W4-T9-M7` survived as recorded, and it was stale before D2: its `new` adds a line that spells the directory name
    the guard looked for before `b2c17f859` (#722, 2026-10-20) renamed its needle, so M7.9 no longer sees it. D2 did not
    cause it: D2's one edit of `docs-parity.test.ts` (`c197b243a`) is a comment. It is re-recorded as
    `NDR-D2-RP-W4-T9-M7`, as D2's other re-recordings are, with its `old` unchanged and a `new` that adds
    `DOCS_SKIP="$HOME/.claude-docs-preview"`, the needle M7.9 reads today: red, 1 failed and 75 passed of 76.
  - The close's "90 earlier rows on files D2 changed do not apply at the tip" is corrected in place to 71. The review
    and the ruling gave 91, the close's 90 and `W3-T8-M12`. This reading measures 71: the close's 90 held the 22 rows
    above, whose files it misread, and 68 rows that did not apply at D2's base either, two of them rows that D1 moved
    and re-recorded (`NDR-D1-RP-L11-T3-M3` and `NDR-D1-RP-W8A-T11-M2`); fix round 1's census change brings three more
    stale rows into scope (`W3-T8-M12`, `S45` and `RLB-T4-M2`); none of the 71 applied at D2's base `8ff15803e`. The
    other 28 earlier rows that do not apply at the tip, 99 in all, are the originals of the rows D2's own edits moved,
    each re-recorded.
  - The close's "(374)" keeps its number, with a note in place. Read this way at the close's tip `71dd65cd4`, the
    definition held 26 rows that the close's reading missed: A6's two, which fix round 1 added, and 24 of the 25 above
    (all but `S44`, which came into scope with fix round 1's census change).
  - Read the same way, the first item's enumeration (rows on a file D1 changed, applying at D1's tip `201ed3587`, plans
    read at `ad47d42d7`) is unchanged: 235 earlier rows in scope, 219 applying and 16 not.
- F3 (minor), `997b0679f`. §4.1's Focus row now reads "the reader's own commands still focus the frame", word for word
  as §6 Keys and M8.1 read, under the same amendment mark. "Synchronously" is in none of the three.
- F4 (minor), the coordinator's ruling: no new reds. D-8730 is a recorded departure, not a guarantee, and a red on the
  focus drop would pin the very gap the operator is told about. D-8730's text is unchanged. A red pins: back keeping
  focus across the swap (`NDR-D2-T13-HEAD-BACKKEY`, and `-BACKKEY-B` in Chrome); the swap closing the panel
  (`NDR-D2-T13-HEAD-SWAPCLOSE`); the open panel's crumbs (`NDR-D2-T13FR2-PANEL-KEY`); and the truth line's two controls
  (`NDR-D2-FR1-F1-TRUTH-KEY`). Descriptive only, with no red:
  - (a) focus on Share, Export or Page details dropping to the page body at the swap;
  - (b) the open panel's GitHub and Open committed version, which React draws anew because the row and the bar draw the
    panel's tail with two components;
  - (c) the refocus ways out that D-8730 excludes, beyond back: mount-then-refocus is pinned for back only (the `focus`
    spy), not for the panel or the row's controls;
  - (d) the two 2026-11-06 phone blocks staying byte for byte, a diff fact each review checks (`git diff` over
    `pwa/src/docs/docs.css` from D2's base is empty).
- F5 (minor), `5cb1ae803`. M8.1's row now says that the commands clause is the library's behaviour in released 1.1.0,
  whose commands focus the frame themselves, and that no row here reds it.
- F6 (minor). (a) `1cd9e13e5`: the ring walk's describe title names `@ccrc/ui` with the other shapes; the rows that
  select the block ("stays ring L1") still match, 9 passed and 68 skipped of 77. (b) F1 above: each control is its own
  case. (c) Fix round 1's A3 note said its planted control finds the import "two hops down"; it is one hop
  (`policy.ts` to `links.ts`), with the specifier as the second step (corrected in place).
- Rows at `ad47d42d7`: the 45 end-state rows whose tests read a file this round changed (16 on the census and 29 on
  `reader-policy.test.ts`), F1's three new rows and the 25 rows new to the table, 73 in all, one at a time as before:
  every one red. The 45 rows' lines above carry the new counts. So the end-state table holds 592 rows (fix round 1's
  564, the 25 above and F1's three), and by each row's last run all 592 are red.
- Suites at the round's code tip `ad47d42d7`:
  - pwa `tsc --noEmit` rc 0 (it covers `src`, `test` and `browser`);
  - every docs and reader unit file (`test/docs-*` and `test/reader-*`) and `browser-dist.test.ts`: 48 of 49 files
    passed, `reader-policy.test.ts` (this round's one pwa change, a describe title) among them, and 1 979 of 1 981
    cases. The two others are planted cases of `docs-scans.test.ts` (M4.H15 and M4.H4) that ran past their 30 s bound.
    Run alone at a one-minute load of 5.8, three planted cases ran 31 to 34 s (Task 9's srcdoc string the third); with
    the run's bound raised to 120 s, all but Task 9's passed, and that case carries its own 30 s bound (36 s). Each
    planted case copies `pwa/src` and parses it whole, the box's CPU pressure stood at about one half (PSI "some", 39 to
    56 per cent), and the test slice's memory cap killed processes during each of these runs. The file's inputs

    PR's runs take it on their own runner (**local**);
  - the census and the reader lab door's own server files (reader-lab-routes, -runs and -vocabulary, box-token-census,

    the plan-text guards run again after this commit;
  - `reader-frame.browser.test.ts` alone, five times (a pwa test file changed; desktop Chromium `chromium-1248`,

**Carries closed at P1 (run 2743, 2026-11-19; ask 600, the coordinator's binding carries).** D2's two minors, measured
once, on X2's wave P1 branch.
- `W4-T10-M10` re-measured once at `1626ae837` (its code is `main` at `0b96bd0dc`): the doubled assignment in
  `ccd/ccrc-doctor-checks` reds `docs-parity.test.ts` with 2 failed and 74 passed of 76, the two M7.3 doctor-half cases
  ("is assigned on exactly one line, and its value is DOCS_PAGE_PREFIX" and "the quoted prefix appears once in the whole
  file: the assignment"). The third name the row carried, "H.redact gives the same answer, with no python rule table", is
  not red, so it is dropped from the row's line in the fence above, as review run 2610 measured. The file was restored
  from a saved copy (its sha256 `e6f58c1f…` before and after).
- D-8730's two clauses, its text unchanged:
  - "the next Tab follows the one bar's order": **descriptive only.** A case asserts it,
    `reader-frame.browser.test.ts`'s "phone-born, back" (its last expectation: one Tab lands on the next stop of the
    one bar's DOM order, computed before the press). The two rows that red that case, `NDR-D2-T13-HEAD-BACKKEY-B`
    and `NDR-D2-T13FR2-FOCUS-LATE`, each red it at an earlier expectation (the very same element holding the focus,
    and the focus a moment later), so no row reds the Tab step itself.
  - "the one bar's layout and order are unchanged": **descriptive only.** Its order is asserted by
    `docs-one-bar.test.tsx`'s "a ready frame on ccrc's bar turns the row into the one bar: back, the title, Quick
    jump, Contents", which no recorded row reds; its layout rests on the two 2026-11-06 phone blocks staying byte for
    byte, the diff fact each review checks (F4's (d) above), and no test reds a change to them.

## Where things stand

### Agreed

- One remaining delivery line in this programme; reuse approved choices and both phone blocks.
- A includes real Settings access; B keeps its approved two-run split; W7 is last.
- Implementation, review, landing, release, installed evidence and retirement are distinct facts.

### Waiting on you

- Do not repeat approval/signing/phone evidence or the recorded frozen-snapshot choice. Required follow-on designs still owe access-model/comment/security decisions and any genuine parity/state conflict; raise only operator-owned choices as precise console items when needed. Reserved rollout, device and retirement actions also wait for exact entry evidence; all unblocked work proceeds autonomously.

### Later

- Only the design §7 explicit accepted/deferred/non-goal rows. No unowned “follow-up” may be used to erase a required delivery task.
